On Wednesday, September 23rd, we received the soil Borings report from g2 consulting group the full report was attached in your Council packets but I'll just cover some of the highlights of the report they Indicated the presence of peat which is a soft soil that contains high amounts Of organic materials that are unsuitable for foundation or floor slab support at The depths of seven and four feet at the two drilling locations additionally There was groundwater detected at seven and seven and a half feet in the two Locations. They indicated that if we were to remove the peat from the site and We'd have to dewater it which could negatively affect the surrounding roads And buildings and things like that. So their recommendation was to utilize the parcel as public parking. The case went to court and the court decided in the favor of the artists. So basically, the gist of it was, if you're not using the image to profit. So like let's say this is just an example the DDA were to print up a bunch of pictures of the Grand Trunk mural and sell them for $50 at the farmers market. The artist then could have a case that says, Hey, you're profiting off of my original work. You can't do that. However, if you have something in place that says we're going to use this for educational and promotional purposes, the artist is aware of it. Then you kind of avoid that issue because the agreement is reached beforehand. So, the purpose of this presentation is to comply with PA 57 of 2018 which is the legislation that not only enable DDAs, but kind of offers the guidance for them. This presentation is purely for informational purposes if there are any questions, kindly ask that you hold them till the end and I'll be happy to answer them. So here we have the DDA boundaries as you can see on this map and the South Lyon DDA district was established in 2001. Next is the DDA law as I mentioned it's PA 57 of 2018. All DDAs in the state of Michigan are governed by the provisions of this act. This law replaces the original DDA law, which was PA 197 of 1975. And should anybody wish to find copies online, you can find them at michiganlegislature .org, and you can search for PA 57 of 2018.

[0:05:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=joltaHgo1OY&t=325s) The composition of the DDA Board of Directors and its staff is listed here. The DDA consists of a nine -member board whose makeup Is a mix of downtown residents, business owners, and property owners.

[0:05:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=joltaHgo1OY&t=341s) The DDA meetings take place They take place the second Thursday of each month at 8am meetings have been held virtually and will be held virtually due to the pandemic. Once in person meetings can resume, they'll take place at City Hall, and they end by 10am. So next we have the DDA budget. Budget. The first thing to talk about is how is the DDA funded. So the DDA's primary source of revenue is called tax increment financing. And what tax increment financing is, is that it's a capture of an increase in property tax value over the year, over the base Year the DDA was established so in the case of southline that was in 2001 and this is due to New investment building sales and building rehabilitations for example and or inflation So as property values rise in the DDA district over the base year the DDA captures the increase In value and invest those funds back into the district so a tiff is not a new tax it's just The Downtown Development Authority capturing the increase in property values and then putting that back into the downtown. Here's a quick look at the DDA's revenue as of June 30, 2021. As you can see, the TIF that was collected for the last fiscal year is $48 ,496. We also had delinquent 2019 and 2020 taxes come in in the amount of $6 ,356 for a total TIF capture of $54 ,862. And then there Were some also some other farmers market sponsor fees and vendor fees as well as Patronicity grant funds for a total revenue of seventy five thousand thirty nine dollars The DDA expenditures as of june 30th 2021 as you can see the grant expenditures line item which Is halfway down on the page was the largest item that we had expenditures for in the budget at Eleven thousand nine ninety two uh the farmers market was the second highest item that we had Expenditures for over the course of our fiscal year the

[0:07:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=joltaHgo1OY&t=472s) Seasonal improvements those were things Such as the garland that went up in the downtown the corn stocks that went on the light poles And things things of that nature so next is the DDA's budget summary for the fiscal year end June 30th uh revenues and expenditures you can see on your screen and for the net of revenues and expenditures is $40 ,628. Next is the South Lyon DDA downtown management philosophy. So what we utilize is the National Main Street four -point approach, which is a subsidiary of the National Trust for Historic Preservation. And those four points are organization, design, promotions, and economic vitality. And I'll get into each of these and how the DDA kind of utilizes these Different uh points of the main street program to improve the downtown so just a quick a little bit About the main street approach uh the one thing that I found uh data for in 2017 they were able To show that for every one dollar public money invested in main street communities that leveraged Over 26 26 of private investment so it's a massive return on investment so the first point I'm going To touch on is organization and organization involves creating a strong foundation for a Sustainable revitalization effort including cultivating partnerships community involvement And resources for the district the the organizations you see on the screen are Vital to the success of the DDA they offer professional networks and opportunities For other down yeah networks and networking opportunities For other downtown management professionals and avenue to grow And additionally to enhance the DDA's organizational capacity We have applied for and been accepted to the associate level in the main street oakland county Program this is the second of three tiers in the program it is eventually a gold mine to have us Move up to the select level of the main street program and I've spoken with members of the County's main street team and they'll be making a visit to south lion I believe in early january To perform an assessment to kind of finalize us moving up in the program. So the next point is design. And design supports the community's transformation by enhancing the Physical and visual assets that set the commercial district apart. So one of the biggest things that We had happen this year was the mural, as you can see here, and its different phases of completion. So we established a mural incentive program that seeks to incentivize downtown property owners To have a mural installed on the side of their building by offsetting some of the costs of the mural. The mural that was done on the Grand Trunk Home received funds from the DDA, and further, the Cultural Arts Commission also provided funding for this project. And between the DDA and Cultural Arts funding, there was a 50 % match for the cost of the mural. Additionally, we are successful in receiving $1 ,500 in grant funds to go towards this mural program. So this is one of the big, you know, kind of transformational things for our downtown. And, you know, because of this mural I've had probably three or four different property owners reach out to me and say, hey, I want to do a mural next year. So we're going to continue to work on hopefully getting some more high quality public art up in our downtown. town. So another kind of cool thing that the design was able to do, the DDA's design committee kind of tackled this pocket park between Bob's Barbershop and the 12 Kitchen Elves. You can see The before and after photos here, but their first project was to revamp this pocket park and make it, You know, more of a welcoming place for people to come and sit if they're either having a coffee, Waiting to get their hair cut, or if they just want to have a nice place to come and come and sit downtown. The project was done for, I think, a total of $500. So it was, you know, a great project Had a great impact on the downtown and people are frequenting this park more often.

[0:11:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=joltaHgo1OY&t=715s) The third point of the Main Street program is economic vitality. And economic vitality focuses On capital incentives and other economic and financial tools to assist new and existing Investing businesses, catalyze property development, and create a supportive environment For entrepreneurs and innovators that drive local economies. So there was a number of new businesses that opened downtown this year, many of which can be seen on your screen. The RCA Building, as you can see in the bottom right, is not open yet. However, they have plans to open in 2022, but I wanted to include them on there because it's a huge project and they've been Doing a lot of work. So we're very excited to have these new businesses open in downtown, Downtown, and you know it's just great to see that people want to invest in downtown Southline. And finally, the fourth point of the main street approach is promotion, which positions the downtown is the center of the community and hub of economic activity, while creating a positive images image that showcases the community's unique Characteristics. So the biggest thing for us and communities across the state was that 2021 saw the return of many Southline events after a hiatus in 2020. With great attendance numbers for each of these events, it was clear the community showed its support of our downtown events that took place over the year. The DDA continues to assess how to improve the quality of events offered downtown and, you know, continually trying to make things better. So as you can see from all the different flyers that I've posted here, we had the Both Ladies' Nights were back, we tried out a food truck rally, the Lake Street Cruise -In, those were back, Pumpkin Fest was back, Cool Yule got a new name, the Holiday Spectacular, and we had the Downtown Trick or Treat.

We have also hired a new farmers market manager officially. Her name is Diana Regan. I spoke With her today she's got all the food trucks scheduled for every saturday throughout the Season as well as 17 vendors signed up already so she's off to a great start the apartment Development on lafayette street is looking to go to Planning Commission soon for site plan Approval so once that's on their agenda we'll obviously know more know more about that and Finally the flower baskets we're going to be taking the leftover baskets from last year out To donahue farms and they're going to get them planted this week so if there's any other questions I'll be happy to answer them

### Monday, May 23, 2022

[Meeting page](../../meetings/2022/2022-05-23-1.md) · [Full transcript](../../transcripts/2022/2022-05-23-1.md) · [Video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IprFdDCcP3M) · South Lyon City Council May 23 2022 part 1

[0:07:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IprFdDCcP3M&t=462s) All right. Good evening, Mayor and Council. First off, I wanted to thank Gene Carroll for his years of service to the DDA Board. He had to step away due to personal obligations, so I just wanted to publicly thank him for his time spent on the DDA Board. He was an architect, which is something that is very valuable to our organization. Moving on, we had flowers planted at the Pocket Park on Sunday, so that area is getting spruced Up again. That's going to be looking good again this year. And then last week, Monday through Wednesday, I attended the National Main Street Conference in Richmond, Virginia. I'll be giving a brief presentation on some of the topics that I learned at the next City Council meeting, but I just wanted to briefly talk about that. Overall, I found it to be hugely beneficial. Not only was I able to learn what other communities are doing, best practices in economic development, downtown development, but I was able to network with Main Street directors from all over the country. I talked with people from Montana, Florida, Virginia, obviously as we were there, Arizona, California, so it was good, it was very good. Next, the flower baskets for downtown and Paul Baker Park will be delivered this week, probably Thursday or Friday, so we can expect those to be going up. The farmer's market over the weekend was moderately successful due to the heavy rain Saturday morning, so we had a few people stick it out, but we're looking to keep the success going in the market. And then finally, the Planning Commission voted to deny preliminary site plan approval for the proposed development at 345 -405 North Lafayette, which is just south of Pullum Windows. It was slated to be a mixed -use development on the fringe of downtown with 2 ,500 square feet of retail frontage and 34 one - and two -bedroom market -rate apartments. City staff spent months working with the developer to get the project to match as closely as possible. What the master plan shows given the conditions present at the site there would have been about 70 people within walking distance of the core downtown which would lift up all aspects of the Downtown including restaurants bars retail establishments the farmers market and other Service providers that call our downtown home additionally it would have helped ease some of The housing shortage in the area particularly for those priced out of the housing market currently I'm somewhat disappointed as this may have as this may be a missed opportunity for high quality Development in the downtown fringe with that that's the end of my report and I'll answer

capture is the year 2000 so what that means is any property tax increases going forward from the year 2000 the DDA captures that increase in the property taxes so it's not an additional tax on the businesses it's just the DDA captures the increase in The property taxes over the course of time and they reinvest that money back into the DDA district That's how that's how tax increment financing works now if you were to expand a DDA district Or alter the boundaries or do anything like that that opens up a whole other process where you Where the other taxing jurisdictions so you have so the DDA captures tax dollars from the city from The county from the library and we do not capture any school taxes once you open that up it allows These other taxing entities to either opt out or kind of enter into a negotiation with the DDA to say hey you know you're actually not going to Capture these tax dollars or they could just opt out entirely so expanding the District is not only a ton of work [0:20:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CV9lfK0FVA4&t=1229s) all right on July 21st 2020 governor Whitmer signed House bill 5781 creating The social district permit which allows a local government to designate a social District within their jurisdiction businesses that are granted a social District permit permit may sell alcohol alcoholic liquor beer wine mixed spirits Or mixed drinks on their licensed premises to customers who may then consume the alcoholic Liquor within the commons area of the social district. Local governments may designate a social district that contains a commons area. During the June 26, 2023 meeting, the City Council Voted to approve the resolution establishing the sidecar social district and the management and Maintenance plans for the district. Additionally, the City Council passed the first reading of an ordinance to of an ordinance amendment of the alcoholic liquor chapter of the Code of ordinances to allow for the consumption of alcoholic beverages in The sidecar social district therefore in order for the social district to come into effect a second reading of the city of South Lyons alcoholic liquor code Shall be amended to allow for the consumption of alcoholic beverages in The sidecar social district
